- **pyterrier-basics**: Core PyTerrier knowledge that every other pyterrier-* skill builds on: how PyTerrier works (transformers over query and result DataFrames), installing pyterrier and Java, the data model (qid, query, docno, score, rank), pipeline operators (&gt;&gt;, %, +, **, |, &), pt.apply, writing a custom pt.Transformer, replacing legacy APIs (pt.init, BatchRetrieve) with their 1.x forms, and the checklist for building a retrieval pipeline with a reranker. Use whenever writing, fixing or reviewing any PyTerrier code, even when another pyterrier skill also applies. ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-basics))
- **pyterrier-caching**: Caches expensive parts of PyTerrier pipelines with pyterrier-caching so repeated runs, experiments and parameter sweeps don't recompute them: reranker and scorer scores (ScorerCache, DenseScorerCache), first-stage results (RetrieverCache), document encodings and other indexing-pipeline output such as SPLADE or doc2query (IndexerCache), and any deterministic column-to-column transform (KeyValueCache), plus Lazy model loading and prebuilt caches from Hugging Face. Use when a pipeline stage is slow and will run more than once, when replacing the removed ~ caching operator, or when loading a cach… ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-caching))
- **pyterrier-colbert**: Uses ColBERT v2 late-interaction models in PyTerrier with pyterrier_colbert2, in every role: loading a prebuilt PLAID index from Hugging Face, building a ColBERT v2 index, end-to-end retrieval with PLAID settings (ncells, centroid_score_threshold, ndocs), reranking any first stage with the index-free text_scorer, and ColBERT pseudo-relevance feedback (PLAID-PRF) with its weighting schemes. Also covers pyterrier-pylate, the PyLate backend for multi-vector models such as GTE-ModernColBERT: encoders, a PlaidIndex artifact, retrieval, and index or text scorers. Covers install on GPU and CPU, the… ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-colbert))
- **pyterrier-dense**: Uses dense bi-encoder models in PyTerrier with pyterrier-dr, in every role: encoders (TAS-B, E5, RetroMAE, GTR, TCT-ColBERT, BGE-M3, any sentence-transformers model), indexing vectors in a FlexIndex, exact and approximate retrieval (numpy, torch, FAISS flat/HNSW/IVF), reranking any first stage with the same model as a scorer, and vector pseudo-relevance feedback (VectorPrf, AveragePrf). Covers matching query and document encoders, similarity functions and prebuilt .flex indexes. Use whenever dense retrieval, bi-encoders, embeddings, FlexIndex or pyterrier_dr appears in a PyTerrier task, wheth… ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-dense))
- **pyterrier-expansion**: Expands queries and documents in PyTerrier: pseudo-relevance feedback and query expansion (RM3, Bo1, KL), proximity rewriting with the sequential dependence model (SDM), interpolating rewritten and original queries, and index-time document expansion with doc2query and Doc2Query-- (generating queries for each document, scoring and filtering them). Use when adding query expansion, PRF, query rewriting or document expansion to a retrieval pipeline, or when choosing between them. Not for first-stage indexing in general, reranking or evaluation. For ColBERT PRF, use pyterrier-colbert. ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-expansion))
- **pyterrier-experiments**: Loads IR test collections and evaluates PyTerrier pipelines: pt.get_dataset and ir_datasets ids, topics and qrels, pt.Experiment with ir_measures metrics (nDCG@10, AP, RR@10, recall), significance tests with multiple-testing correction, per-query results, saved runs, TREC DL / MS MARCO / Robust04 / BEIR conventions, whole benchmark suites with SuiteEval (BEIR, NanoBEIR, LoTTE, BRIGHT), and parameter tuning with GridSearch. Use when asked to evaluate, benchmark or compare retrieval runs, compute metrics, test significance, tune parameters, or load a dataset in PyTerrier, even if pt.Experiment… ([lionisakis/pyterrier-skills](https://github.com/lionisakis/pyterrier-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/pyterrier-experiments))
